{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.04306pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 8.04 0 L 10.96 2.86 L 11.38 2.49 L 9.23 0 L 11.38 -2.49 L 10.96 -2.86 L 8.04 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\hskip 0.43057pt}}{\mathrm{L}})
to reach a proof of
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma,w\!:\!\varphi\mathrel{\hskip 0.43057pt\hbox to8.96pt{\vbox to4.18pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.71042pt\lower-2.0882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.55972pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -0.6 0 L 8.94 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.04306pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 8.04 0 L 10.96 2.86 L 11.38 2.49 L 9.23 0 L 11.38 -2.49 L 10.96 -2.86 L 8.04 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\hskip 0.43057pt}\psi\vdash\Delta
of the adequate height.

(\exists{\mathrm{L}}):
then the last rule has the following form.

\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},w^{\prime}\!:\!y,\Gamma,w^{\prime}\!:\!\varphi(y/x)\vdash\Delta

(\exists{\mathrm{L}})

\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma,w^{\prime}\!:\!\exists x\varphi\vdash\Delta

If the principal formula is not the one we intend to modify, then we can use the induction hypothesis in the premise and reapply the rule.
If the principal formula is the labeled formula we intend to modify, then we have that
u=w^{\prime}
.
First, we can apply Lemma
37
on the premise to obtain a proof of
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},w\!:\!y,u\!:\!y,\Gamma,u\!:\!\varphi(y/x)\vdash\Delta
. Then, as
w\twoheadrightarrow^{*}_{\mathcal{R}}u
we apply Lemma
34
to obtain a proof of
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},w\!:\!y,\Gamma,u\!:\!\varphi(y/x)\vdash\Delta
. Finally, as all previous lemmas preserve height, it suffices to apply the induction hypothesis on this proof to obtain a proof of
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},w\!:\!y,\Gamma,w\!:\!\varphi(y/x)\vdash\Delta
. A simple application of the rule
(\exists{\mathrm{L}})
reaches our goal.

(\forall{\mathrm{L}}):
then the last rule has the following form.

\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma,w^{\prime}\!:\!\forall x\varphi,u^{\prime}\!:\!\varphi(t/x)\vdash\Delta

(\forall{\mathrm{L}})

\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma,w^{\prime}\!:\!\forall x\varphi\vdash\Delta

If the principal formula is not the one we intend to modify, then we can use the induction hypothesis in the premise and reapply the rule.
If the principal formula is the labeled formula we intend to modify, then we have that
u=w^{\prime}
.
Then, we can apply the induction hypothesis on the premise to obtain a proof of
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma,w\!:\!\forall x\varphi,u^{\prime}\!:\!\varphi(t/x)\vdash\Delta
. Note that we have
w\twoheadrightarrow^{*}_{\mathcal{R}}u^{\prime}
as
w\twoheadrightarrow^{*}_{\mathcal{R}}u
and
u\twoheadrightarrow^{*}_{\mathcal{R}}u^{\prime}
. So, we can apply the rule
(\forall{\mathrm{L}})
to reach our goal.

∎

For each of the rules in the following lemma, we obtain a proof via an usual induction on the height of proofs.

Lemma 41
.

The rules
(\land{\mathrm{L}}),(\land{\mathrm{R}}),(\lor{\mathrm{L}}),(\lor{\mathrm{R}})
are hp-invertible in
\mathsf{LBIQ}(\mathcal{C})
.

Lemma 45
.

The rules
(ctr_{l})
and
(ctr_{r})
are hp-admissible in
\mathsf{LBIQ}(\mathcal{C})
.

Proof.

We simultaneously prove the hp-admissibility of the two rules. We reason by primary induction on the structure of
\varphi
(PIH) and secondary induction on the height of derivations (SIH). In each case, we consider the last rule applied, and whether
\varphi
is principal in that rule. We omit the cases of initial rules as they are straightforward.

We start by the rule
(ctr_{l})
. If
\varphi
is not principal in the last rule, then we proceed as usual by applying SIH on
\varphi
in the premises of the rule, and then the rule. Note that all rules which have their principal formula on the right fall in this category.

If
\varphi
is principal, then for the rules
(\land{\mathrm{L}})
and
(\lor{\mathrm{L}})
we use the invertibility lemmas proved previously, jointly with PIH. For the rules in which the principal formula is not deleted in the premises, i.e.
({\rightarrow}{\mathrm{L}})
and
(\forall{\mathrm{L}})
, we simply use SIH to contract the principal formula. We give the case of
({\rightarrow}{\mathrm{L}})
as an example.

({\rightarrow}{\mathrm{L}}):
then the last rule has the following form, where
\varphi=\chi\rightarrow\psi
.

\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma,w\!:\!\chi\rightarrow\psi,w\!:\!\chi\rightarrow\psi\vdash\Delta,u\!:\!\chi

\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma,w\!:\!\chi\rightarrow\psi,w\!:\!\chi\rightarrow\psi,u\!:\!\psi\vdash\Delta

({\rightarrow}{\mathrm{L}})

\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma,w\!:\!\chi\rightarrow\psi,w\!:\!\chi\rightarrow\psi\vdash\Delta

We can simply apply SIH on both premises to obtain proofs of
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma,w\!:\!\chi\rightarrow\psi\vdash\Delta,u\!:\!\chi
and
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma,w\!:\!\chi\rightarrow\psi,u\!:\!\psi\vdash\Delta
. A simple application of
({\rightarrow}{\mathrm{R}})
gives us the desired result.

Finally, we are left with the rules
({\mathrel{\hskip 0.43057pt\hbox to8.96pt{\vbox to4.18pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.71042pt\lower-2.0882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.55972pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

and
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma,u\!:\!\psi\vdash\Delta,w\!:\!\chi\mathrel{\hskip 0.43057pt\hbox to8.96pt{\vbox to4.18pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.71042pt\lower-2.0882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.55972pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

. A simple application of the rule gives us the desired result.

Finally, we are left with the rules
({\rightarrow}{\mathrm{R}})
and
(\forall{\mathrm{R}})
which we treat individually.

({\rightarrow}{\mathrm{R}}):
then the last rule has the following form, where
\varphi=\chi\rightarrow\psi
.

\mathcal{R},{w}R{u},\mathcal{T},\Gamma,u\!:\!\chi\vdash\Delta,u\!:\!\psi,w\!:\!\chi\rightarrow\psi

({\rightarrow}{\mathrm{R}})

\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma\vdash\Delta,w\!:\!\chi\rightarrow\psi,w\!:\!\chi\rightarrow\psi

Then, we proceed as follows.

\mathcal{R},{w}R{u},\mathcal{T},\Gamma,u\!:\!\chi\vdash\Delta,u\!:\!\psi,w\!:\!\chi\rightarrow\psi

Lem.
43

\mathcal{R},{w}R{u},{w}R{v},\mathcal{T},\Gamma,u\!:\!\chi,v\!:\!\chi\vdash\Delta,u\!:\!\psi,v\!:\!\psi

Lem.
32

\mathcal{R},{w}R{u},{u}R{v},\mathcal{T},\Gamma,u\!:\!\chi,v\!:\!\chi\vdash\Delta,u\!:\!\psi,v\!:\!\psi

Lem.
33

\mathcal{R},{w}R{u},\mathcal{T},\Gamma,u\!:\!\chi,u\!:\!\chi\vdash\Delta,u\!:\!\psi,u\!:\!\psi

PIH

\mathcal{R},{w}R{u},\mathcal{T},\Gamma,u\!:\!\chi\vdash\Delta,u\!:\!\psi

({\rightarrow}{\mathrm{R}})

\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma\vdash\Delta,w\!:\!\chi\rightarrow\psi

First, we use the invertibility Lemma
43
on the initial premise. Second, we use Lemma
32
to push the branch
{w}R{v}
to
u
and obtain
{u}R{v}
. Third, we merge the points
v
and
u
using Lemma
33
. Once this is done, we can apply the induction hypothesis PIH and reapply the rule.

(\forall{\mathrm{R}}):
then the last rule has the following form, where
\varphi=\forall x\psi
.

\mathcal{R},{w}R{u},\mathcal{T},u\!:\!y,\Gamma\vdash\Delta,u\!:\!\psi(y/x),w\!:\!\forall x\psi

(\forall{\mathrm{R}})

\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma\vdash\Delta,w\!:\!\forall x\psi,w\!:\!\forall x\psi

Then, we proceed as follows.

\mathcal{R},{w}R{u},\mathcal{T},u\!:\!y,\Gamma\vdash\Delta,u\!:\!\psi(y/x),w\!:\!\forall x\psi

Lem.
44

\mathcal{R},{w}R{u},{w}R{v},\mathcal{T},u\!:\!y,v\!:\!z,\Gamma\vdash\Delta,u\!:\!\psi(y/x),v\!:\!\psi(z/x)

Lem.
32

\mathcal{R},{w}R{u},{u}R{v},\mathcal{T},u\!:\!y,v\!:\!z,\Gamma\vdash\Delta,u\!:\!\psi(y/x),v\!:\!\psi(z/x)

Lem.
33

\mathcal{R},{w}R{u},\mathcal{T},u\!:\!y,u\!:\!z,\Gamma\vdash\Delta,u\!:\!\psi(y/x),u\!:\!\psi(z/x)

Lem.
35

\mathcal{R},{w}R{u},\mathcal{T},u\!:\!y,u\!:\!y,\Gamma\vdash\Delta,u\!:\!\psi(y/x),u\!:\!\psi(y/x)

Lem.
34

\mathcal{R},{w}R{u},\mathcal{T},u\!:\!y,\Gamma\vdash\Delta,u\!:\!\psi(y/x),u\!:\!\psi(y/x)

PIH

\mathcal{R},{w}R{u},\mathcal{T},u\!:\!y,\Gamma\vdash\Delta,u\!:\!\psi(y/x)

(\forall{\mathrm{R}})

\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma\vdash\Delta,w\!:\!\forall x\psi

First, we use the invertibility Lemma
44
on the initial premise. Second, we use Lemma
32
to push the branch
{w}R{v}
to
u
and obtain
{u}R{v}
. Third, we merge the points
v
and
u
using Lemma
33
. Fourth, we use Lemma
35
to rename the (fresh) variable
z
to
y
. Fifth, we contract the labeled variable
w\!:\!y
using Lemma
34
. Once this is done, we can apply the induction hypothesis PIH and reapply the rule.
∎

Theorem
30

(Cut-elimination)
.

The
(cut)
rule is admissible in
\mathsf{LBIQ}(\mathcal{C})
.

Proof.

We proceed by a primary induction (PIH) on the complexity of the cut formula, and a secondary induction (SIH) on the sum of the heights of the proofs of the premises of
(cut)
. Assume that we have proofs of the following form, with
w\twoheadrightarrow^{*}_{\mathcal{R}}u
.

\pi_{1}

r_{1}

\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma\vdash\Delta,w\!:\!\varphi

\pi_{2}

r_{2}

\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma,u\!:\!\varphi\vdash\Delta

We prove that there is a proof of
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma\vdash\Delta
by a case distinction on
r_{1}
and
r_{2}
, the last rules applied in the above proofs.

(I)

\mathbf{r_{1}}=(ax):
Then
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma\vdash\Delta,w\!:\!\varphi
is of the form
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma_{0},v_{0}\!:\!p(\vec{t})\vdash\Delta_{0},v_{1}\!:\!p(\vec{t})
where
v_{0}\twoheadrightarrow^{*}_{\mathcal{R}}v_{1}
.
If
v_{1}\!:\!p(\vec{t})
is
w\!:\!\varphi
, then we have that
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma,u\!:\!\varphi\vdash\Delta
is of the form
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma_{0},v_{0}\!:\!p(\vec{t}),u\!:\!p(\vec{t})\vdash\Delta
where
\Gamma=\Gamma_{0},v_{0}\!:\!p(\vec{t})
. Given that
v_{0}\twoheadrightarrow^{*}_{\mathcal{R}}v_{1}
and
v_{1}\twoheadrightarrow^{*}_{\mathcal{R}}u
, we can apply Lemma
40
on the latter to obtain a proof of
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma_{0},v_{0}\!:\!p(\vec{t}),v_{0}\!:\!p(\vec{t})\vdash\Delta
. Consequently, we obtain a proof of
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma_{0},v_{0}\!:\!p(\vec{t})\vdash\Delta
, i.e. of
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma\vdash\Delta
, using Lemma
45
.
If
v_{1}\!:\!p(\vec{t})
is not
w\!:\!\varphi
, then we have that
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma\vdash\Delta
is of the form
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma_{0},v_{0}\!:\!p(\vec{t})\vdash\Delta_{0},v_{1}\!:\!p(\vec{t})
where
v_{0}\twoheadrightarrow^{*}_{\mathcal{R}}v_{1}
. The latter is easily provable using the rule
(ax)
.

(II)

\mathbf{r_{1}=(\bot{\mathrm{L}}):}
Then
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma\vdash\Delta,w\!:\!\varphi
is of the form
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma_{0},v\!:\!\bot\vdash\Delta,w\!:\!\varphi
where
\Gamma=\Gamma_{0},v\!:\!\bot
. Consequently, we know that that
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma\vdash\Delta
is of the form
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma_{0},v\!:\!\bot\vdash\Delta
. We straightforwardly prove the latter via a single application of
(\bot{\mathrm{L}})
.

(III)

\mathbf{r_{1}=(\top{\mathrm{R}}):}
Then
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma\vdash\Delta,w\!:\!\varphi
is of the form
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma\vdash\Delta_{0},v\!:\!\top
. If
w\!:\!\varphi
is
v\!:\!\top
, then we have that
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma,u\!:\!\varphi\vdash\Delta
is of the form
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma,u\!:\!\top\vdash\Delta
. Thus, we obtain a proof of
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma\vdash\Delta
using Lemma
38
. If
w\!:\!\varphi
is not
v\!:\!\top
, then we have that
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma\vdash\Delta
is of the form
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma\vdash\Delta_{0},v\!:\!\top
. A single application of
(\top{\mathrm{R}})
gets us to our goal.

(IV)

\mathbf{r_{1}}=(ds):
Then
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma\vdash\Delta,w\!:\!\varphi
is of the form
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma_{0},v\!:\!p(\vec{t})\vdash\Delta,w\!:\!\varphi
and we have a proof of
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},v\!:\!VT(\vec{t}),\Gamma_{0},v\!:\!p(\vec{t})\vdash\Delta,w\!:\!\varphi
.
Consequently, we know that
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma\vdash\Delta
is of the form
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma_{0},v\!:\!p(\vec{t})\vdash\Delta
. We also have that
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma,u\!:\!\varphi\vdash\Delta
is of the form
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma_{0},v\!:\!p(\vec{t}),u\!:\!\varphi\vdash\Delta
.
We can apply Lemma
37
repetitively on the proof of the latter to obtain a proof of

\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},v\!:\!VT(\vec{t}),\Gamma_{0},v\!:\!p(\vec{t}),u\!:\!\varphi\vdash\Delta
which we call
S
, while preserving height. Then, we proceed as follows.

\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},v\!:\!VT(\vec{t}),\Gamma_{0},v\!:\!p(\vec{t})\vdash\Delta,w\!:\!\varphi

S

SIH

\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},v\!:\!VT(\vec{t}),\Gamma_{0},v\!:\!p(\vec{t})\vdash\Delta

(ds)

\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma_{0},v\!:\!p(\vec{t})\vdash\Delta

Note that the instance of SIH is justified because the sum of the heights of the proofs of the premises has decreased.

(V)

\mathbf{r_{1}=(\land{\mathrm{L}}):}
Then
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma\vdash\Delta,w\!:\!\varphi
is of the form
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma_{0},v\!:\!\psi\land\chi\vdash\Delta,w\!:\!\varphi
and we have a proof of
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma_{0},v\!:\!\psi,v\!:\!\chi\vdash\Delta,w\!:\!\varphi
. Consequently, we know that
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma\vdash\Delta
is of the form
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma_{0},v\!:\!\psi\land\chi\vdash\Delta
. We also have that
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma,u\!:\!\varphi\vdash\Delta
is of the form
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma_{0},v\!:\!\psi\land\chi,u\!:\!\varphi\vdash\Delta
. We apply Lemma
41
on the proof of the latter sequent to obtain a proof of
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma_{0},v\!:\!\psi,v\!:\!\chi,u\!:\!\varphi\vdash\Delta
preserving height. Thus, we proceed as follows.

\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma_{0},v\!:\!\psi,v\!:\!\chi\vdash\Delta,w\!:\!\varphi

\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma_{0},v\!:\!\psi,v\!:\!\chi,u\!:\!\varphi\vdash\Delta

SIH

\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma_{0},v\!:\!\psi,v\!:\!\chi\vdash\Delta

(\land{\mathrm{L}})

\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma_{0},v\!:\!\psi\land\chi\vdash\Delta

Note that the instance of SIH is justified as the sum of the heights of the proofs of its premises is smaller than the one of the initial cut.

(VI)

\mathbf{r_{1}=(\land{\mathrm{R}}):}
Then
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma\vdash\Delta,w\!:\!\varphi
is of the form
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma\vdash\Delta_{0},v\!:\!\psi\land\chi
and we have proofs of
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma\vdash\Delta_{0},v\!:\!\psi
and
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma\vdash\Delta_{0},v\!:\!\chi
.
If
w\!:\!\varphi
is
v\!:\!\psi\land\chi
, then we have proofs of

(VII)

\mathbf{r_{1}=(\lor{\mathrm{L}}):}
Then
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma\vdash\Delta,w\!:\!\varphi

and

. Consequently, we know that
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma\vdash\Delta
is of the form
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma_{0},v\!:\!\psi\lor\chi\vdash\Delta
. We also have that
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma,u\!:\!\varphi\vdash\Delta
is of the form

. We apply Lemma
41
on the proof of the latter sequent to obtain proofs of
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma_{0},v\!:\!\psi,u\!:\!\varphi\vdash\Delta
and
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma_{0},v\!:\!\chi,u\!:\!\varphi\vdash\Delta
preserving height. Thus, we proceed as follows where
\pi
is the first proof.

(IX)

\mathbf{r_{1}=({\rightarrow}{\mathrm{L}}):}
Then
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma\vdash\Delta,w\!:\!\varphi
is of the form

and

such that
v\twoheadrightarrow^{*}_{\mathcal{R}}v_{0}
. Consequently, we know that
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma\vdash\Delta
is of the form
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma_{0},v\!:\!\psi\rightarrow\chi\vdash\Delta
. We also have that
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma,u\!:\!\varphi\vdash\Delta
is of the form
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma_{0},v\!:\!\psi\rightarrow\chi,u\!:\!\varphi\vdash\Delta
. Given that
v\twoheadrightarrow^{*}_{\mathcal{R}}v
, we apply Lemma
42
on the proof of the latter sequent to obtain proofs of
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma_{0},v\!:\!\psi\rightarrow\chi,u\!:\!\varphi\vdash\Delta,v\!:\!\psi
, which we call
S_{0}
, and
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma_{0},v\!:\!\psi\rightarrow\chi,v\!:\!\chi,u\!:\!\varphi\vdash\Delta
, which we call
S_{1}
, preserving height. Thus, we proceed as follows where
\pi
is the first proof displayed.

Note that both instances of SIH are justified as the sum of the heights of the proofs of their premises is smaller than the one of the initial cut.

, then we have a proof of

is of the form

. In this case, we need to consider the shape of
r_{2}
. If

is not principal in
r_{2}
, then we apply the hp-invertibility of
r_{2}
to the proof of
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma\vdash\Delta,v\!:\!\psi\rightarrow\chi
and invoke SIH with the resulting proof to cut
u\!:\!\psi\rightarrow\chi
from the premises of
r_{2}
, and then reapply
r_{2}
to reach our goal. If
u\!:\!\psi\rightarrow\chi
is principal in
r_{2}
, then the premises of
r_{2}
are of the shape
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma,u\!:\!\psi\rightarrow\chi\vdash\Delta,v_{1}\!:\!\psi
and
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma,u\!:\!\psi\rightarrow\chi,v_{1}\!:\!\chi\vdash\Delta
where
u\twoheadrightarrow^{*}_{\mathcal{R}}v_{1}
. Note that by the shape of the original cut we know that
w\twoheadrightarrow^{*}_{\mathcal{R}}u
and by our assumption that
w\!:\!\varphi
is
v\!:\!\psi\rightarrow\chi
, we have that
w=v
. Therefore,
v\twoheadrightarrow^{*}_{\mathcal{R}}v_{1}
since
v\twoheadrightarrow^{*}_{\mathcal{R}}u
and
u\twoheadrightarrow^{*}_{\mathcal{R}}v_{1}
, meaning we can proceed as follows where
\pi_{0}
and

(XIII)

\mathbf{r_{1}=(\exists{\mathrm{L}}):}
Then
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma\vdash\Delta,w\!:\!\varphi
is of the form
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma_{0},v\!:\!\exists x\psi\vdash\Delta,w\!:\!\varphi
and we a have proof of
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},v\!:\!x,\Gamma_{0},v\!:\!\psi(y/x)\vdash\Delta,w\!:\!\varphi
, which we call
S
. Consequently, we know that
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma\vdash\Delta
is of the form
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma_{0},v\!:\!\exists\psi\vdash\Delta
. We also have that
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma,u\!:\!\varphi\vdash\Delta
is of the form
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma_{0},v\!:\!\exists x\psi,u\!:\!\varphi\vdash\Delta
.
We apply Lemma
44
on the proof of the latter sequent to obtain a proof of
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},v\!:\!z,\Gamma_{0},v\!:\!\psi(z/x),u\!:\!\varphi\vdash\Delta
. Thus, we proceed as follows.

S

\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},v\!:\!z,\Gamma_{0},v\!:\!\psi(z/x),u\!:\!\varphi\vdash\Delta

Lem.
35

\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},v\!:\!y,\Gamma_{0},v\!:\!\psi(y/x),u\!:\!\varphi\vdash\Delta

SIH

\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},v\!:\!y,\Gamma_{0},v\!:\!\psi(y/x)\vdash\Delta

(\exists{\mathrm{L}})

\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma_{0},v\!:\!\exists\psi\vdash\Delta

Note that the instance of SIH is justified as the sum of the heights of the proofs of the premises is smaller than the one of the initial cut.

(XIV)

\mathbf{r_{1}=(\exists{\mathrm{R}}):}
Then
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma\vdash\Delta,w\!:\!\varphi
is of the form
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma\vdash\Delta_{0},v\!:\!\exists x\psi
and we have a proof of
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma\vdash\Delta_{0},v\!:\!\exists x\psi,v\!:\!\psi(t/x)
where
t
is available for
v
.

If
w\!:\!\varphi
is not
v\!:\!\exists x\psi
, then we have a proof of
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma\vdash\Delta_{1},v\!:\!\exists x\psi,v\!:\!\psi(t/x),w\!:\!\varphi
, which we call
S
, and
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma,u\!:\!\varphi\vdash\Delta
is of the form
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma,u\!:\!\varphi\vdash\Delta_{1},v\!:\!\exists x\psi
. Then, we proceed as follows.

S

\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma,u\!:\!\varphi\vdash\Delta_{1},v\!:\!\exists x\psi

Lem.
42

\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma,u\!:\!\varphi\vdash\Delta_{1},v\!:\!\exists x\psi,v\!:\!\psi(t/x)

SIH

\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma\vdash\Delta_{1},v\!:\!\exists x\psi,v\!:\!\psi(t/x)

(\exists{\mathrm{R}})

\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma\vdash\Delta_{1},v\!:\!\exists x\psi

If
w\!:\!\varphi
is
v\!:\!\exists x\psi
, then we have proof a of
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma\vdash\Delta,v\!:\!\exists x\psi,v\!:\!\psi(t/x)
, and
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma,u\!:\!\varphi\vdash\Delta
is of the form
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma,u\!:\!\exists x\psi\vdash\Delta
. In this case, we need to consider the shape of
r_{2}
. If
u\!:\!\exists x\psi
is not principal in
r_{2}
, then we apply the hp-invertibility of
r_{2}
to the proof of
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma\vdash\Delta,v\!:\!\exists x\psi
and then invoke SIH with the resulting proof to cut
u\!:\!\exists x\psi
with the premises of
r_{2}
, and then reapply
r_{2}
to reach our goal. If
u\!:\!\exists x\psi
is principal in
r_{2}
, then the premise of

(XV)

\mathbf{r_{1}=(\forall{\mathrm{L}}):}
Then
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma\vdash\Delta,w\!:\!\varphi
is of the form
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma_{0},v\!:\!\forall x\psi\vdash\Delta,w\!:\!\varphi
and we a have proof of
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma_{0},v\!:\!\forall x\psi,v_{0}\!:\!\psi(t/x)\vdash\Delta,w\!:\!\varphi
where
v\twoheadrightarrow^{*}_{\mathcal{R}}v_{0}
and
t
is available for
v_{0}
. Consequently, we know that
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma\vdash\Delta
is of the form
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma_{0},v\!:\!\forall x\psi\vdash\Delta
. We also have that
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma,u\!:\!\varphi\vdash\Delta
is of the form
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma_{0},v\!:\!\forall x\psi,u\!:\!\varphi\vdash\Delta
.
We apply Lemma
42
on the proof of the latter sequent to obtain a proof of
\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma_{0},v\!:\!\forall x\psi,v_{0}\!:\!\psi(t/x),u\!:\!\varphi\vdash\Delta
, which we call
S
. Thus, we proceed as follows.

\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma_{0},v\!:\!\forall x\psi,v_{0}\!:\!\psi(t/x)\vdash\Delta,w\!:\!\varphi

S

\mathcal{R},\mathcal{T},\Gamma_{0},v\!:\!\forall x\psi,v_{0}\!:\!\psi(t/x)\vdash\Delta

Note that the instance of SIH is justified as the sum of the heights of the proofs of the premises is smaller than the one of the initial cut.
